Anthropic closes 17% higher as global AI shares slide

Category: AI

Anthropic, the maker of Claude and the first major frontier-AI lab to trade publicly, closed its Nasdaq debut Thursday at $97.17, up 17% from its $83 offering price after opening at $111.50 [11][8]. The stock briefly implied a fully diluted valuation above $2 trillion before surrendering much of its early gain in a volatile session [11][27]. The debut followed an $81.3 billion share sale, the largest IPO on record, and gave public investors their first direct stake in a company built primarily on generative AI [8][49]. U.S. indexes fell more than 2%, and Asian technology shares slid Friday as investors continued to question AI valuations, infrastructure spending and the path to profits [109][129][94]. Anthropic chief executive Dario Amodei said the company would pace AI development to keep systems safe and under human control [104]. As a public company, Anthropic will report quarterly on revenue and the costs of computing and model development [93][65].

Primary Source: View original article

If we can show it’s possible to make the right choices on safety and still build an incredibly successful company, others in our industry will be inspired to do the same.

— Dario Amodei, Anthropic chief executive (TechCrunch)

Did you know: Anthropic’s founders have pledged 80% of their personal company equity to charitable causes, but the pledge is voluntary, revocable and not legally binding [97].

Key Points

  • Retail trading: Individual investors traded $772 million of Anthropic shares on debut day, a record in Vanda Research data dating to 2012, but net purchases were $60 million, below SpaceX’s $118 million first-day total [32].
  • Limited float: Only 7.4% of Anthropic’s shares were available for public trading, or up to 9.8% including employee-held Class A shares, according to Renaissance Capital [79].
  • Public oversight: Anthropic’s founders retain 50.1% of general shareholder voting power, while an independent trust has authority to appoint a majority of directors [125].
  • Business model: Anthropic says price cuts tied to newer models and customer discounts reduced revenue growth by about $600 million [107].
  • Revenue growth: Anthropic reported $33.24 billion in revenue for the first nine months of 2026, up 1,200% from a year earlier [27].
  • Asian selloff: On Friday, Taiwan’s Taiex fell 6.2% and was down 17.5% for the week, while South Korea’s Kospi lost 5.2% and Japan’s Nikkei dropped 5% [129].
  • Safety governance: Anthropic’s long-term benefit trust can elect or remove a majority of the board, but it does not manage day-to-day operations; a Future of Life Institute AI and national-security lead said the trust may have little influence over routine decisions [207][70].
  • Infrastructure bills: Anthropic has committed to more than $900 billion in vendor payments over 19 years, mostly for data centers, including $70 billion due over the next 12 months; it also has $6.2 billion in debt [25][59][151].
  • Founder control: The founders’ Founder LLC holds a single Class F share carrying 50.1% of voting rights, subject to a condition that they collectively retain at least 2% of their Class A holdings [252].
  • Insider supply: Up to 316 million shares held by non-founder insiders could be sold on the first day without lockups, while the stock traded 846 million shares during the session [86].
  • Revenue growth: The company reported $16.96 billion in third-quarter revenue, while annualized revenue based on recent usage reached $81 billion at October’s end; more than 11,900 customers spend over $100,000 [242].
  • Compute efficiency: Anthropic says compute multipliers can stack as successive gains, and its filing anticipates Claude may eventually identify such improvements itself [107].
  • Employee wealth: The average employee’s equity could be worth about $44 million at the $83 IPO price, based on SEC filings and investor estimates [58].

Perspectives

Jed Ellerbroek, Argent Capital portfolio manager: Ellerbroek cautioned against treating the session’s price moves as a clear verdict on the AI sector, saying investors were shifting portfolios to include Anthropic and that data-center construction could become more expensive and slower [109].

Sources: Reuters

Jacob Coxon, former Anthropic researcher: Coxon, speaking at a protest outside Anthropic’s San Francisco headquarters, said he objected to powerful AI being developed by for-profit corporations and warned that rapid advances could outpace understanding of the systems [121].

Sources: AFP

Daniela Amodei, Anthropic president and co-founder: She said the company established an AI safety team before releasing its first product and chose not to include ads in Claude. She described the initial public offering as a way to give more people a stake in building AI properly [63].

Dario Amodei, Anthropic CEO: Amodei said the company would pace the training and deployment of AI systems until it has confidence they are safe and remain under human control [189].

Sources: PBS NewsHour

Bob O'Donnell, chief analyst at TECHnalysis Research: O'Donnell said the debut was only an initial signal, pointing to the future performance of OpenAI and Anthropic, Nvidia developments and local politics around data centers as factors still affecting market confidence [11].

Sources: Reuters

Mason County United, a group opposing a West Virginia data center project: Member Elaine Matheny questioned whether the data center’s land and resource costs were justified, saying, “Do we really need to cover up all our farmland so Claude can do all our thinking for us?” [25].

Sources: WIRED

Rebecca Walser, chief investment officer at Walser Wealth Management: Walser advised investors against chasing the IPO and said she expected the shares to face pressure in coming weeks, including around former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang’s Senate testimony [36].

Sources: CNBC

Timeline

  • 2021: Former OpenAI researchers founded Anthropic [11][8]
  • 2023: Anthropic launched Claude commercially, beginning its enterprise AI business [33]
  • 18 November 2026: Anthropic priced 980 million IPO shares at $83 each [11][8]
  • 19 November 2026: Shares opened at $111.50 in their Nasdaq debut and closed at $97.17 [11]
  • 20 November 2026: Asian markets fell as AI-linked stocks extended their selloff [129][94]
  • 2021: Former OpenAI researchers founded Anthropic with a stated safety focus [14]
  • September 2026: Amodei outlined proposals for pacing development and independent safety evaluation [181]
  • 19 November 2026: Anthropic began trading publicly on Nasdaq under ticker ANT [181]
  • 2021: Former OpenAI employees founded Anthropic [41].
  • June 2026: SpaceX raised about $75 billion in its record-setting public offering [41].
  • 18 November 2026: Anthropic priced its IPO at $83 per share, above its previously targeted $70-to-$78 range [41].
  • 20 November 2026: Anthropic shares fell nearly 5% in after-hours trading, though they remained above the IPO price [50].

Technical Details

  • Non-voting placement: Anthropic sold $26 billion in Class N shares to technology companies in a private placement; the shares carry no voting rights, distinguishing them from the publicly offered Class A stock [97].

Industry Impact

  • Venture capital: The public listing converted early private investments into publicly traded stakes, with Reuters reporting that venture firms had invested billions in later funding rounds [69].
  • AI pricing: Analysts say Anthropic’s IPO proceeds could give it the financial capacity to undercut Chinese AI rivals, while those rivals’ open-weight models may appeal to businesses seeking greater control over deployment and data [162].
  • AI chipmakers: Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix, which make memory chips used in AI data centers, each fell more than 5% in Friday trading; Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company dropped more than 4% [50].
  • Cloud and chip suppliers: Anthropic disclosed $900.9 billion in aggregate computing commitments, including $70.7 billion due in the next year [68].
  • AI infrastructure: Anthropic has committed to paying vendors more than $900 billion over 19 years, mostly for data centers that power Claude and its other models [25].

Historical Background

Anthropic’s public benefit corporation structure allows it to weigh societal impacts alongside shareholder interests [25]. Its seven co-founders have pledged 80% of their personal equity to philanthropy, though the pledges are voluntary and revocable [104].

Business Angle

  • Open-weight models accounted for 19% of enterprise AI use, up from 11% a year earlier [57].
  • Customer concentration: The five largest customers generated about a quarter of nine-month revenue, though no individual customer accounted for more than 10% [aa.com#1].
  • More than 80% of Anthropic’s revenue came from models introduced in the prior six months, according to the Financial Times [57].
  • Revenue mix: Usage-based revenue rose $24.2 billion year over year in the first nine months of 2026, compared with a $6.5 billion increase in subscription revenue [aa.com#1].
  • Amazon and Google are both major Anthropic investors and infrastructure suppliers, linking its funding and cloud spending to the same technology partners [97].

Q&A

Q: Why do estimates of Anthropic’s market value differ by hundreds of billions of dollars?

A: A fully diluted valuation counts shares that could exist if employee options and restricted stock units become shares; a basic market capitalization uses currently outstanding shares. Reuters said Anthropic’s fully diluted value included unvested employee awards, which is why estimates differ [11][122].
